A RIq
A color parameter which stores a vector, used to create the moon phase in the sky material. This is essentially the direction of the sun light, relative to the moon's texture space.
A function to apply a consolve variable only when necessary, to avoid setting cvars that are already set as desired.
A lookup table containing all the star locations (Right Ascension(Degrees) in R channel, Declination(Degrees) + 90 in G channel) which are used by the real stars sprites system.
A lookup table which defines the profile of the cloud shapes with cloud coverage. Use the "Cloud Profile Authoring Tool" to make your own profile LUT textures. That utility is found in Blueprints/Tools.
A multiplier for 2D dynamic cloud density at the top of the sky. Also affects the cloud shadows
A multiplier for 2D dynamic cloud density close to the horizon.
A multiplier for 2D dynamic cloud density in the immediate area around the moon.
A multiplier for 2D dynamic cloud density in the immediate area around the sun.
A multiplier for overall fog density at night.
A multiplier for overall fog density in the day.
A normalized distance to the center of the framebuffer where the effect takes place.
A padding 0 for the hour can be included or left out without issue. AM and PM letters are case insensitive.
A simple adjustment of the sky light's color temperature, applied regardless of the sky light mode.
A simple contrast boost for the sky material and clouds
A simple saturation adjustment for the sky and lighting
A time offset for the clouds. Useful to fine tune a specific formation when working with a Cloud Speed of 0, or to keyframe cloud movement directly.
A tint color applied in the middle of the terminator. Useful for planets with atmospheres like Earth, to imitate the way light filters warm along the terminator.
A tint color for the lens flare effect
A value in degrees, representing the current time of day, where each day is a 360 degree rotation
A value of 0 means the last of this day of the week that month. So if DST starts on the last sunday of the month, this would be 0.
A value of 0 means the last of this day of the week that month. So if ends starts on the last sunday of the month, this would be 0.
A very simple large scale variation which varies cloud density in big patches across the sky. Increase to have heavier variation, decrease to reduce variation.

Active Cache Group
Active Properties
Active Update Speed
Active Update Speed determines how often active properties on things like materials and rendering components are updated. In game, it's determined based on how fast the time and weather variables are changing. It ranges from 0 to 3. You can increase this variable to keep the active update speed from going to its lower levels.

Allows brightening indirect lighting by calculating material diffuse color for indirect lighting as pow(DiffuseColor, 1 / DiffuseColorBoost). Values above 1 (original diffuse color) aren't physically correct, but they can be useful as an art direction knob to increase the amount of bounced light in the scene. Best to keep below 2 as it also causes reflections to be brighter than the scene.

CallbackTarget
Called Starting Event Dispatchers
Called at midnight every day that passes at runtime.
Called at the beginning of every hour, and is called for hours which are skipped over by an instant change of time. For example, if time skips from 4:30 to 7:30, this dispatcher will be called for 5, 6, and 7.
Called at the start of every hour, and does NOT get called for hours which are skipped by instant changes of time. For example, if time skips from 4:30 to 7:30, this dispatcher will only be called for 7.
Called when Time of Day changes by more than 100 in a single update
Called when the minute (derived from Time of Day) changes. Outputs the minute as an integer
Called when the sun drops below the horizon. Can be adjusted to trigger earlier or later using Sunset Event Offset.
Called when the sun rises above the horizon. Can be adjusted to happen earlier or later using Sunrise Event Offset.
Called when the transition started by "Transition Time of Day" is finished.
Called whenever Time of Day passes one of the values in the Custom Time Dispatchers float array, in the Animate Time of Day category. Outputs the index of the value in that array which caused the call.
Called whenever the Year/Month/Day on UDS changes, from any source.
Calls Sunset and Sunrise as part of construction script, so that actors which bind to these events as part of their construction script can be affected in editor.
Calls the midnight dispatcher and offsets the current date forward

Control Sky Atmosphere Settings
Control over the grain intensity in the mid-tone region of the image.
Control over the grain intensity in the regions of the image considered highlight areas.
Control over the grain intensity in the regions of the image considered shadow areas.
Control the intensity of the color (hue) for the highlights region of the image.  Higher values will result in more vibrant colors.
Control the intensity of the color(hue) for the entire image.Higher values will result in more vibrant colors.
Control the intensity of the colors (hue) in the mid-tone region of the image.  Higher values will result in more vibrant colors.
Control the intensity of the colors (hue) in the shadow region of the image.  Higher values will result in more vibrant colors.
Control the luminance curve of the highlight region. Raising or lowering this value will result brightening or darkening the mid-tones of the highlight region.
Control the luminance curve of the mid-tone region of the image. Raising or lowering this value will result brightening or darkening the mid-tones of the image.
Control the luminance curve of the scene. Raising or lowering this value will result brightening or darkening the mid-tones of the entire image.
Control the luminance curve of the shadow region. Raising or lowering this value will result brightening or darkening the mid-tones of the shadow region.
Control the range of light and dark values in the highlights region. Lower values will reduce the difference between bright and dark areas while higher values will increase the difference between the bright and dark areas.
Control the range of light and dark values in the mid-tone region. Lower values will reduce the difference between bright and dark areas while higher values will increase the difference between the bright and dark areas.
Control the range of light and dark values in your scene. Lower values will reduce the difference between bright and dark areas while higher values will increase the difference between the bright and dark areas.
Controlled by the wind on Ultra Dynamic Weather if it exists.
Controls "Indirect Lighting Intensity" on the moon directional light component
Controls "Indirect Lighting Intensity" on the sun directional light component
Controls Fog Max Opacity based on the camera's position relative to the volumetric cloud layer. Above the cloud layer it will be completely disabled.
Controls how Lumen rays are lit when Lumen is using Hardware Ray Tracing.  By default, Lumen uses the Surface Cache for best performance, but can be set to 'Hit Lighting' for higher quality.
Controls how much Dust Amount will attenuate directional light from the sun and moon.
Controls how much Lumen Final Gather is allowed to cache lighting results to improve performance.  Larger scales cause lighting changes to propagate faster, but increase GPU cost and noise.
Controls how much Lumen Scene is allowed to cache lighting results to improve performance.  Larger scales cause lighting changes to propagate faster, but increase GPU cost.
Controls how much heavy fog will attenuate directional light from the sun and moon.
Controls how much light rays are cast down through gaps in individual cloud formations. This is in addition to rays cast through gaps in overall cloud coverage. Increases performance cost.
Controls how much the 3D Noise moves vertically with cloud movement
Controls if "cast shadows" will ever be enabled on the moon directional light.
Controls if "cast shadows" will ever be enabled on the sun directional light.
Controls if static clouds opacity will fade down when cloud coverage goes below a partly cloudy level down to 0.
Controls if the moon renders in front or behind the objects in the space layer.
Controls the Absorption Intensity of the Sky Atmosphere component. Primarily affects how much of the Sunset/Sunrise Color (Absorption) is applied when the sun or moon is low in the sky.
Controls the color of the Night Sky Glow.
Controls the color of the light contributed by Light Pollution Intensity.
Controls the color of the scene along the magenta - green axis (orthogonal to the color temperature).  This feature is equivalent to color tint in digital cameras.
Controls the color of the sun disk (scaled by the sun light color), with the height of the sun. The input for this curve is a 0 to 1 range, with 0 being the sun pointing straight up, and 1 being the sun pointing straght down.
Controls the color temperature or white balance in degrees Kelvin which the scene considers as white light.
Controls the contrast of the dark end of the tonemapper curve. Larger values increase contrast and smaller values decrease contrast.
Controls the distance from a receiving surface where skylight leaking reaches its full intensity.  Smaller values make the skylight leaking flatter, while larger values create an Ambient Occlusion effect.
Controls the distance of the scene depth fade in the light ray sprite material.
Controls the height of the tonemapper curve.  Raising this value can cause bright values to more quickly approach fully-saturated white.
Controls the intensity (brightness) of the lens flare.
Controls the maximum distance that Lumen should trace while solving lighting.  Values that are too small will cause lighting to leak into large caves, while values that are large will increase GPU cost.
Controls the minimum and maximum level of scene brightness that the auto exposure will adjust to.
Controls the overall steepness of the tonemapper curve.  Larger values increase scene contrast and smaller values reduce contrast.
Controls the saturation of the volumetric cloud ambient light.
Controls the size of instances that can be represented in Lumen Scene.  Larger values will ensure small objects are represented, but increase GPU cost.
Controls the size of the film grain. Size of texel of FilmGrainTexture on screen.
Controls the size of the soft falloff for the edge of the water fog values
Controls the softness/sharpness of the sun disk.
Controls the strength of image sharpening applied during tonemapping.
Controls what fraction of the skylight intensity should be allowed to leak.  This can be useful as an art direction knob (non-physically based) to keep indoor areas from going fully black.
Controls whether or not the loop will skip over hidden enumeration values.
Controls which lighting channels on the directional light are enabled, when the directional light is above the horizon.

Determines based on its height if the sun should currently be casting shadows. Also disables shadows if cloud shadows have completely occluded the light, if the option for that is enabled.
Determines how directional multiscattering remains after successive octaves of scattering.
Determines how the nebula will be sorted on top/behind the planets/moons in the space layer
Determines how the occlusion values are acquired. Either by sending out line traces to test for collision, testing the occlusion location for occlusion volumes (UDS_Occlusion_Volume) or both.
Determines how the system will behave at runtime. Game / Real-time mode allows the system to optimize and spread out some of its runtime calculations, to reduce load on the game thread. Cinematic / Offline mode bypasses these optimizations so that nearly everything on the sky does a complete update every frame at runtime. Cinematic mode also automatically adjusts some quality settings at runtime for maximum quality while rendering. See the Cinematic/Offline Rendering category for settings to adjust the specifics of those quality adjustments.
Determines if this decal will move with the sun, moon, or not move.
Determines if this space object will move with the sun, moon, or not move.
Determines the Lit Intensity parameter, which scales the brightness of the base sky color, cloud wisps and 2D dynamic clouds. Basically everything except the nighttime elements like moon, stars and aurora.
Determines the albedo/color of the volumetric clouds, using the setting for volumetric cloud color, and scaling with Overall Intensity and Directional Balance.
Determines the amount of ray particles rendered in the grid. A lower number will be denser, and heavier on performance.
Determines the cloud density parameter for the cloud shadows, based on Cloud Coverage.
Determines the current Max Trace Distance for the volumetric clouds, depending on the camera's position within the cloud layer.
Determines the current density for the height fog component, based on weather, time and exposed settings.
Determines the current height falloff for the height fog component, based on fog, cloud coverage and dust.
Determines the current height of the cloud layer, as scaled by cloud coverage (the layer is thinner with lower cloud coverage)
Determines the density value used for the volumetric cloud material, based on Cloud Coverage
Determines the extinction scale (volumetric density) of the top and bottom of the cloud layer.
Determines the light source angle of the sun or moon directional light.
Determines the multiscattering occlusion for the volumetric clouds. How much light is occluded as it passes through the layer.
Determines the source of fog colors. Read the tooltip for each mode for more information.
Determines the source of sky/cloud colors
Determines the stars color parameter used by the sky material. Scales with stars intensity.
Determines the sun disk intensity, as used in the sky material to scale the sun color.
Determines the type of clouds/aurora rendered
Determines the value used for the sky atmosphere component's Height Fog Contribution.
Determines view sample scale with day and night values and scaled by material quality.

E`:K
Each element in this array is an additional cloud layer. These layers are rendered relative to the main layer (either above or below it), and are essentially adjusted copies of the main layer.
Each entry in this array will be a planet/moon rendered in the sky layer (behind the clouds/aurora, on top of stars, moon, sun). Note, this additional rendering into the sky layer makes use of the Dbuffer, so it will only work if Dbuffer Decals are enabled and supported.
Each entry in this array will be a post process component.
Each entry needs a valid material selected for it to be rendered. This material should be a Deferred Decal material which outputs to Base Color.
Each value in this array will cause the "Custom Time" event dispatcher to be called at that time of day, outputting the index in this array which caused the call.

For a dispatcher that does get called for skipped hours, use the Hourly dispatcher.
For a dispatcher that doesn't include those skipped hours, use Current Hour Changed instead.
For adjusting the behavior of the Sunrise event dispatcher. Offset the exact time that the dispatcher will be called, relative to the actual moment the sun comes up. No effect if Use System Time is true.
For adjusting the behavior of the Sunset event dispatcher. Offset the exact time that the dispatcher will be called, relative to the actual moment the sun goes below the horizon.  No effect if Use System Time is true.
For depth of field to use the hair depth for computing circle of confusion size. Otherwise use an interpolated distance between the hair depth and the scene depth based on the hair coverage (default).
For example, set to 50, the total number of voxels would be 50x50x10x10, so 250,000.
For materials this needs to be the "PostProcess" domain type. This can be used for any UObject object implementing the IBlendableInterface (e.g. could be used to fade weather settings).
For the server, updates the replicated time values, for the clients to recieve and use to increment time until the next update.
For tiling stars, scales how many times they tile across the sky
For turning the exposure settings on/off at runtime
For use in scenes where time, weather, and the look of the sky and lighting never change. This disables almost all of the tick logic on UDS and UDW, as an optimization for levels that have no need for dynamics. Understand, if this is enabled, none of the features which change time, weather, or update the effect of any properties at runtime will work. UDW will be limited to only the one global weather state it starts with. Actors like Weather Override Volumes or Radial Storms will not work.

If "Aurora Moves with Time of Day" is true, scales how much time contributes movement
If "Clouds Move with Time Of Day" is enabled, this scales the amount that Time of Day contributes to cloud movement.
If "Move Sky Light Capture with Camera Location" is enabled, this toggles if the Z axis of the camera position is also used for the update.
If "Replicate Time of Day to Clients" is true, defines how frequently the clients are updated with the server's Time of Day.
If Apply Water Fog Values is true, this will be the albedo of the fog below the Water Level set in the Water Level category.
If Apply Water Fog Values is true, this will be the extinction of the fog below the Water Level set in the Water Level category.
If DBuffer Supported
If Dim Directional Lights With Cloud Coverage is enabled, this defines the start and end points of Cloud Coverage which the dimming is applied across
If Dim Directional Lights With Cloud Coverage is enabled, this is the exponent of the 0 to 1 range defined by the lower and upper cloud coverage bounds.
If Interior Adjustments are active, Blend Weight will be turned down when the player camera is inside an interior.
If Interior Adjustments are active, Blend Weight will be turned down when the player camera is outside/unoccluded.
If a Sky Configuration asset is selected here (authored using the Configuration Manager) that asset will be used to control all the UDS settings on this instance.
If a directional light actor is selected for this, it will be used in place of the built in directional light component for the moon.
If a directional light actor is selected for this, it will be used in place of the built in directional light component for the sun.
If a mesh is selected here, it will be used for the sky sphere mesh instead of the one built for UDS's sky material.
If a sky light actor is selected for this, it will be used in place of the built in sky light component.
If an exponential height fog actor is selected for this, it will be used in place of the built in exponential height fog component.
If checked, Blend Weight will be turned down when Dust Amount is high.
If checked, Blend Weight will be turned down when Dust Amount is low.
If checked, Blend Weight will be turned down when Fog is high.
If checked, Blend Weight will be turned down when Fog is low.
If checked, Blend Weight will be turned down when it's dawn/dusk.
If checked, Blend Weight will be turned down when it's night time.
If checked, Blend Weight will be turned down when its daytime.
If checked, Blend Weight will be turned down when rain is active on UDW (ignored if UDW is not used)
If checked, Blend Weight will be turned down when rain is inactive on UDW (ignored if UDW is not used)
If checked, Blend Weight will be turned down when snow is active on UDW (ignored if UDW is not used)
If checked, Blend Weight will be turned down when snow is inactive on UDW (ignored if UDW is not used)
If checked, Blend Weight will be turned down when the sky is mostly clear of clouds.
If checked, Blend Weight will be turned down when the sky is overcast.
If enabled, adds a random starting offset to the cloud formation on begin play. Only applied if Project Mode is game / realtime.
If enabled, all tick logic on UDS and UDW will be staggered to only happen every other frame. How this works depends on if UDS is used alone or alongside UDW:
If enabled, controls the exposure settings on a post process component, to apply basic auto exposure settings with the values below
If enabled, increments the Moon Phase value with time that has passed.
If enabled, removes one of the 2D dynamic cloud layers. This uses a lower complexity material for a small performance gain.
If enabled, sets the time and date to the system time
If enabled, specular scale on the Sun and Moon will turn down when the light is low to the horizon and there is significant cloud coverage. This can help avoid obvious mismatches between cloud shadows and the directional specular of the light, especially on large distant reflective surfaces like an ocean.
If enabled, the Global Volumetric Material will be used to affect fog extinction and albedo below the water level set in the Water Level category.
If enabled, the Global Volumetric Material will sample the global distance field to have fog form in the air immediately above them.
If enabled, the Time of Day and date values will be controlled using the system clock. The time set will be determined by sampling the current UTC time and offsetting it using the Time Zone, and Daylight Savings Time if active
If enabled, the Time of Day will be set to a random value when the level is run.
If enabled, the cvar "r.LightFunctionAtlas.SlotResolution" will be set using the value below.
If enabled, the occlusion collision traces will happen on the GPU, querying the global distance field instead of simple collision. This is done using a special niagara system with GPU particle logic for querying occlusion.
If enabled, the simulation will add a slight offset to sun and moon orientation only when nearing the horizon, to compensate for the flat horizon of unreal's height fog, so that visible sunset/sunrise will happen closer to the exact times they should.
If enabled, the sun's light and sky color will be affected when the sun goes behind the moon, or behind one of the planets in the Space Layer.
If enabled, this controls the cvar "r.LightFunctionAtlas.SlotResolution" to set the resolution of the light function atlas.
If enabled, when the camera goes above the cloud layer, the effects of cloud coverage on things like fog, lighting, sky color, etc, will fade out.
If false, each client will have their own local set of modifiers which can differ from the server. If true, the server will send its modifier state to the clients any time the modifier functions are called.
If half rate tick is enabled, it will only apply when framerate is above this threshold, to keep the optimization from resulting in sky/lighting updates that appear visibly stuttery.
If inside cloud fog is enabled, this acts as a color multiplier for it.
If on, interprets Cloud Coverage on UDS using the old 0-3 range. This exists purely for legacy support, to avoid breaking existing setups from before Cloud Coverage was 0-10 on both UDS and UDW.
If project mode is Cinematic, changes out a value at runtime. Used to modify things like volumetric cloud quality while rendering.
If sun light intensity is higher than default (if using physical values, in the thousands, for example) the light intensity will be dimmed down as the sun goes below the horizon, to ease the transition to twilight lighting and moonlight.
If the Light Vector setting is Custom, this vector will be used for the direction light shades this planet.
If the Sun lens flare is enabled, this option also disables the engine's stock image based flares so the effects don't overlap.
If the directional light is completely occluded everywhere by the cloud shadows (to the point that the directional light is having no visible effect on the lighting) the shadows on the light will be disabled, as an optimization.
If the setting Adjust for Path Tracer is true, and this is enabled, the sky will render a post process material designed to approximate the exponential height fog, to fill in for the fog rendering support the path tracer itself does not have.
If the sky light is capture based, movable, and using real time capture, this option will enable the sky light's position being set to the camera location as it moves. This allows the global reflection environment to be accurate to where you are in the level, with respect to things like the cloud formations in the sky.
If this has a valid object, the material selected will be used instead of the included UDS cloud mats. You could potentially author your own new material driven by the same parameters UDS uses. But I think the most likely use for this will be for a modified duplicate of the UDS cloud material. That material is "Volumetric Clouds" in the Materials folder.
If this is checked, the sky will check the Game User Settings for the current Effects Quality, and use it to set the Sky Mode using the map below.
If this is enabled, Time of Day will move forward at runtime, to create a day/night cycle. If the sun's mobility is not movable, this has no effect.
If this is enabled, directional light intensity will reduce all the way to 0 as cloud coverage gets overcast. The sky color will try to compensate by injecting the current fog color into the clouds/sky. This can be useful to correct for materials and effects which cannot sample the light function used for cloud shadowing.
If this is enabled, the Player Occlusion system (which tests the collision around the player to approximate if they are inside an interior) will be used to apply the property adjustments in the Interior Adjustments category, when the player is occluded inside an interior.
If this is set to 0, the 2D cloud shadows will not offset with the angle of the light, and will appear to be projected straight down from the Z axis.
If this option is checked, you can select the Moon Target widget visible when UDS is selected, and move it around to manually control the location of the moon. If this is unchecked, the moon's location will be determined by time of day and the options below for yaw and pitch, or the Simulation options if using Simulate Real Moon.
If this option is checked, you can select the Sun Target widget visible when UDS is selected, and move it around to manually control the location of the sun. If this is unchecked, the sun's location will be determined by time of day and the options below for yaw and pitch, or the Simulation options if using Simulate Real Sun.
If this setting is enabled, the progress of Time of Day will contribute to cloud movement.
If this value is not 0, the static clouds texture will slowly rotate, with the speed scaled by this value.
If true and Simulate Real Moon is enabled, the sim will control Moon Scale (the angular diameter of the moon) with the simulation.
If true and Simulate Real Sun is enabled, the sim will control Sun Scale (the angular diameter of the sun) with the simulation.
If true, Occlusion Portal components will be used to affect occlusion if available.
If true, UDS will check for some hard incompatibilities with project settings, and show a warning if any are found.
If true, atmosphere color and a ring of atmosphere "glow" will encircle the planet.
If true, clients will periodically sync with the server's Time of Day.
If true, the aurora will move with the progress of Time of Day.
If true, this Timecode represents a Drop Frame timecode used to account for fractional frame rates in NTSC play rates.
If true, uses the Moon Phase Normal texture to render the shading of the moon using the moon phase value. If disabled, the moon texture will show at its full brightness without the phase shading.
If using 2 layers, the distance between the top of the first layer and the bottom of the second layer. In KM. Increasing this will increase the thickness of the full volumetric cloud layer, so it will have an impact on the volumetric render quality and performance.
If using Simulate Real Sun, this setting controls how quickly Time of Day moves forward, if Animate Time of Day is enabled. A value of 1 is roughly realistic speed.
If using a captured sky light with real time capture (a feature mobile doesn't support) this sky light mode will be used as a replacement on mobile platforms.
If using a realtime captured sky light, the sun disk will be enabled in the reflection capture when specular scale is reduced, to stand in for sun reflections as well as it can.
If using the color mode Simplified Color, this curve defines how the directional lights are dimmed as they near the horizon. The input for this curve is the Z channel of the light's forward vector.
If using the color mode Simplified Color, this curve defines how the light color is affected as the sun or moon nears the horizon. The input for this curve is a 0 to 1 range, with 0 being the sun pointing straight up, and 1 being the sun pointing straght down.
If using the color mode Simplified Color, this is the base sky color, before scattering is applied,  at night.
If using the color mode Simplified Color, this is the base sky color, before scattering is applied, at dawn/dusk.
If using the color mode Simplified Color, this is the base sky color, before scattering is applied, during the day.
If using the color mode Simplified Color, this is the color used for 2D and static clouds dark color, at dawn/dusk.
If using the color mode Simplified Color, this is the color used for 2D and static clouds dark color, at night.
If using the color mode Simplified Color, this is the color used for 2D and static clouds dark color, during the day.
If using the color mode Simplified Color, this is the color used for 2D and static clouds light color, at dawn/dusk.
If using the color mode Simplified Color, this is the color used for 2D and static clouds light color, at night.
If using the color mode Simplified Color, this is the color used for 2D and static clouds light color, during the day.
If using the sky modes other than volumetric clouds, the clouds don't have an actual height in the level. However, certain effects, like lightning on UDW, still need to spawn at a specific height. This defines the altitude, relative to UDS, that is used in that case.
If water fog values and the water caustics (controlled from the Water Level category) are both enabled, this scales the amount of the caustic ray effect sampled by the water fog through the global volumetric material.
If you enable any of the "Disable" toggles, its blend weight will be actively updated at runtime with time and weather, based on the settings for each entry. This can be used to apply post processing settings that are specific to when its daytime, or specific to when it's overcast, for example.
If you need to change the sky mode when the game is already running, use this function to do so.
If you need to disable the system's control of a particular cvar, add it to this array and it will not be set by UDS or UDW.
If you select a material here, this material will be used for the sky sphere instead of the UDS sky mat. UDS will still attempt to control material parameters on it the same as it does normally, so you can build a mat which makes use of parameters which are named the same as the UDS material.
If you want to hide weather effects as well, UDW has its own similar Hide Weather function.

Note that adding additional cloud layers increases the GPU cost of cloud rendering.
Note the option in the Aurora category "Use Auroras" which can enable a simpler 2D aurora effect in the background of the other Sky Modes.
Note these values are formatted in hundredths of an hour, just like Time of Day, so for example 9:30 AM would be 950.
Note this would not be called on the client in a multiplayer context.
Note, in Unreal Engine having light sources in constant motion can incur extra rendering costs that have nothing to do with UDS specifically, like the cost of cached virtual shadow maps being redrawn when light sources move. Keep this in mind when making performance considerations in a game with a dynamic time of day.
Note, this only applies at runtime. When previewing the effects of player occlusion in the level editor, the simple collision traces will still be used.
Note, values above 1 can cause an engine bug in 5.5 resulting in flickering of the lower hemisphere.

RemovedFromWorld
Removes the 3D texture samples from the volumetric cloud shader when cloud coverage reaches the threshold value below. This is a very significant optimization with almost no reduction in visual quality, because when fully overcast the 3D noise of the clouds is mostly lost anyway.

The caustic pattern will blur as depth below the water level increases, to imitate the effect of the refraction going out of focus and diffusing through the water as distance from the surface increases. This value scales how quickly that happens.
The caustic pattern will blur in areas which are shadowed by the clouds (visible in partial cloud coverage). To imitate the effect of the light source becoming diffused by the cloud, and so creating diffuse caustics. This value scales how much that blur will be applied.
The caustics pattern will blur with camera distance, scaled with this value. This is good for hiding tiling artifacts and aliasing in the distance.
The classes selected here should be for actors which render a water surface.

The max distance rays wll trace through the cloud layer. Used when the camera is not within the altitude range of the cloud layer.
The max distance rays wll trace through the cloud layer. Used when the camera is within the altitude range of the cloud layer.
The max distance that rays will start being cast though the volumetric cloud volume. If you need to render clouds that are further away, try raising this.
The max distance that the traces used for occlusion will test. This should be generally determined by the max size of your interior spaces.
The max distance the clouds will cast shadows onto each other through the volumetric layer. In kilometers.
The max number of samples used to render the 2D (non-volumetric) auroras. This is the number of samples used on a part of the screen where bright/tall auroras are visible. The samples scale downward from there.
The max specular scale of the sun directional light. Is dimmed down as the sky becomes overcast.
The max value for fog start distance, the distance from the camera height fog (not volumetric fog) will start rendering.
The maximum count of refraction event to trace. When hit lighting is used, Translucent meshes will be traced when LumenMaxRefractionBounces > 0, making the reflection tracing more expenssive.
The maximum intensity of the moon directional light, in Lux.
The maximum specular scale of the moon directional light. Further scales down with cloud coverage/fog.
The maximum, unfiltered color of the moon directional light.
The maximum, unfiltered color of the sun directional light.
The min value for fog start distance, the distance from the camera height fog (not volumetric fog) will start rendering.
The minimum projected screen radius for a primitive to be drawn in the velocity pass, percentage of screen width. smaller numbers cause more draw calls, default: 4%
The mip level sampled for the cloud formation texture. (Higher values use a lower resolution/low detail sample)
The mip level sampled from the Aurora Texture for volumetric auroras
The mobility of the sky light component. If selecting Stationary, make sure you have the project setting enabled to support stationary sky lights.
The mobility of the sun directional light component. Must be movable for time of day to be able to change at runtime.
The month daylight savings time ends, if enabled
The month daylight savings time starts, if enabled
The niagara system which renders the individual star sprites when "Real Stars Fade to Sprites When Zoomed" is enabled.
The number of 3D noise samples used for volumetric fog right next to the camera. Determines the max level of detail of the 3D noise.
The number of days since year 1, day 1, month 1 on the selected calendar.
The number of high frequency noise levels will scale with the sample distance from the camera and the field of view. This value clamps how high that number of successive noise samples can go. Will have an effect on cost of cloud rendering, mainly when close up to clouds.
The number of voxel 10x10 grids which compose the total voxel field on the X and Y axes.
The opacity of the 2D dynamic clouds
The opacity of the atmosphere color and glow
The opacity of the wispy static layer of clouds behind the dynamic ones. This value is used when there is medium cloud coverage.
The opacity of the wispy static layer of clouds behind the dynamic ones. This value is used when there is no cloud coverage.
The overall brightness of the sky colors
The overall tiling (scale) of the 2D dynamic cloud textures
The parent materials used to make the Volumetric Cloud dynamic material instances. There's a Simplified and a Complex material, utilized by the feature which shifts to a simplified version when the sky is heavily overcast.
The path tracer renders the sky using a realtime captured sky light cubemap. This sets the resolution of that cubemap. Understand increasing this above 2048 can risk crashing the GPU depending on the hardware and the context.
The pitch of the moon's path. 0 would mean the moon goes to the top of the sky, Increasing values pitch the angle so the moon peaks lower. Ignored if using Simulate Real Moon.
The pitch of the ring's rotation
The post process settings this component will use.
The priority of the post process component. Will determine the order they're applied relative to other components/post process volumes.
The radius of the sharp backlighting around the 2D dynamic clouds
The real time (in minutes) which will pass between sunrise and sunset. The day will always last this long in real time, no matter what Dawn Time and Dusk Time are set to. This setting is not used if Simulate Real Sun is true.
The real time (in minutes) which will pass between sunset and sunrise. The night will always last this long in real time, no matter what Dawn Time and Dusk Time are set to. This setting is not used if Simulate Real Sun is true.

This applies a renderer cvar (r.VolumetricCloud.ApplyFogLate) which fixes some issues at the renderer level with volumetric clouds rendering behind fog.
This can be useful for example if you have UDS instances in many levels which you want to share a configuration that can be centrally edited.
This color is multiplied over the light shaft bloom
This does not apply if Project Mode is set to Cinematic / Offline.
This function will apply the change and rebuild the data necessary to display the change.
This is for if you want to use this planet/moon to render a ring around the local planet (the planet you're on). When this is true, only the ring decal will be rendered, centered around the local planet.
This is the squeeze factor for the DOF, which emulates the properties of anamorphic lenses.
This is used to set the Absorption variable on the Sky Atmosphere component, which mainly has the effect of tinting the sky color when the sun or moon is low in the sky. Can be used in conjuction with the intensity value below to achieve dramatically colored sunsets. Also note that this color isn't delivered directly to the sky atmosphere, but has its hue inverted first. This is because the actual color for absorption will be the opposite of the one visible, because the absorbed light is what the value represents. So this inverting is done for convenience.
This map will be used to determine the sky mode based on Effects Quality, if the "Use Sky Mode Scalability Map" variable is checked. The integer values correspond to the quality levels. 0 is low, 1 is medium, 2 is high, 3 is epic, and 4 is cinematic.
This setting controls engine cvars (r.VolumetricRenderTarget.Mode and r.VolumetricRenderTarget) which determine how volumetric clouds are rendered. It has performance implications and each of these modes also has very different amounts and types of visual artifacting. Be sure to read the tooltips for each and choose a mode which is appropriate for your project.
This setting controls engine cvars which determine how volumetric aurora are rendered. It has performance implications and each of these modes also has very different amounts and types of visual artifacting. Be sure to read the tooltips for each and choose a mode which is appropriate for your project.
This setting does affect the cost of cloud rendering, with larger values costing more, due to adding additional noise samples to more distant clouds.
This toggles Cast Shadows on the sky light if the Mobility is set to Movable. In practice, this just toggles the effect of Distance Field Ambient Occlusion, if enabled by the project configuration.
This value is added to the colors in the highlight region.  Raising or lowering this value will result in the highlights being more or less washed-out.
This value is added to the colors in the mid-tone region of the image.  Raising or lowering this value will result in the mid-tones being more or less washed-out.
This value is added to the colors in the shadow region.  Raising or lowering this value will result in the shadows being more or less washed-out.
This value is added to the colors of the scene.  Raising or lowering this value will result in the image being more or less washed-out.
This value is used at dawn/dusk (applied at full effect when the sun is exactly on the horizon)
This value is used at night.
This value is used during the day.
This value multiplies the colors in the highlight region.  Raising or lowering this value will result in brightening or darkening the affected region.
This value multiplies the colors in the mid-tone region of the image.  Raising or lowering this value will result in brightening or darkening the affected region.
This value multiplies the colors in the shadow region.  Raising or lowering this value will result in brightening or darkening the affected region.
This value multiplies the colors of the image.  Raising or lowering this value will result in brightening or darkening the entire scene.
This value sets the lower threshold for what is considered to be the highlight region of the image.
This value sets the threshold for what is considered to be the shadow region of the image.
This value sets the upper threshold for what is considered to be the highlight region of the image.  This value should be larger than HighlightsMin. Default is 1.0, for backwards compatibility
This value should be set to 1 in most cases.

This will artificially lengthen dawn and dusk by flattening the sun's trajectory around the horizon.  Ignored if using Simulate Real Sun.

WeightedBlendables
Wg;|
When GPU Distance Field Collision is used, this is the number of points on the hemisphere which query the distance field. This defines the amount of directions traced against.
When MegaLights is enabled, other direct lighting algorithms like Deferred Shading will no longer be used, and other shadowing methods like Ray Traced Shadows, Distance Field Shadows and Shadow Maps will no longer be used.
When calculating fog density, this is the value which is started with, and then added to using the contribution values below.
When completely overcast, the clouds view sample scale will be multiplied by this amount. Can reduce cost of cloud rendering when high sample count is unneeded.
When enabled, the UDS actor (and UDW if also in the level) will run some of the same logic that it runs on tick at runtime, in the level editor. This allows things which change with the editor camera location to respond as you move the camera in the level editor, for example. This should also always be enabled if you're working in sequencer and keyframing values on UDS and UDW, so those keyframes can be previewed.
When interpolating the shortest path should be taken.
When using Simulate Real Stars, if this is enabled, the stars will fade from a texture sample over to individual sprites (for the brightest 500k stars in the sky) rendered by a niagara system, when the camera zooms in enough to make that necessary. This allows long/telescopic lenses to show the stars without revealing the limits of the real stars texture resolution.
Whether to use high quality mirror reflections on the front layer of translucent surfaces.  Other layers will use the lower quality Radiance Cache method that can only produce glossy reflections.  Increases GPU cost when enabled.
Whether to use screen space traces for Lumen Global Illumination. Screen space traces bypass Lumen Scene and instead sample Scene Depth and Scene Color. This improves quality, as it bypasses Lumen Scene, but causes view dependent lighting.
Whether to use screen space traces for Lumen Reflections. Screen space traces bypass Lumen Scene and instead sample Scene Depth and Scene Color. This improves quality, as it bypasses Lumen Scene, but causes view dependent lighting.
WhileLoop
White Balance uses the Temperature value to control the virtual camera's White Balance. This is the default selection.

You can enable this to have it disable the directional lights below the horizon, similar to the other sky modes. This could be useful for a scene on a planet/moon with no atmosphere for example.
You can use this option to make your own lens flare. Just create an instance of the Lens Flare material in the Materials folder (or duplicate one of the presets as a starting point.
